### 4.2 利用工具产生关键词创意

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1q34y1r7RA?p=2)　时长 4分46秒

**原文**

Part two, how to generate keyword ideas using keyword research tools. There are quite a few awesome tools that help you study what people are searching for in Google. Let me cover a few of the most popular keyword research tools and explain how they work. So there's this tool called Answer the Public, which is quite popular among SEOs and content marketers. Probably because of this highly charismatic dude on their homepage. Now I'm just kidding, that's a really neat free tool seriously. Give it any word of phrase and it will return a list of relevant questions that people put in the Google. For example, if I type cats into the search bar, it will give me almost 200 questions that contain the word cats. Can cats eat dog food, where cats like to be pet, which cats shed the list, etc. You're probably wondering, how does this tool know what people are searching for? Well, the source of their data is very simple. It's Google's auto-suggest feature. If you start typing your question like, can cats, it will immediately show you some popular searches. And if you start typing the next letter, it will give you suggestions based of this letter. That's where this little free tool gets their search queries. As stated on their homepage, they use auto-suggest feature and Google and Bing to give you keyword suggestions. Here's another similar tool that is based on Google auto-suggest feature. It's called Uber's suggest. The difference between the two is that Uber's suggest doesn't focus on questions. And all their keyword suggestions will start from your seed keyword as you can see here. Everything starts with the word cats. Both of these tools don't show you the monthly search volume of the search queries they suggest, but you already know where to get it, right? Now onto the next keyword research tool, which you probably know already. It's called Google keyword planner and this part of AdWords, Google's advertising platform. This tool used to be the single best source of keyword ideas back in the days, but in recent years, Google was taking more and more data away from SEO professionals. So today, Google keyword planner is a great tool for advertisers that want to pay for traffic from Google, but not so much for SEO professionals who want to get free traffic from Google. And yet, you can still get great keyword ideas from Google keyword planner. If I enter the word cats, I'll get a few hundred cool keyword ideas and the tool can even sort them into groups, which is rather handy and useful. The main downside is that you don't see monthly search volume of these keywords. You only see ranges, which are not super helpful. And if you want to see the actual numbers, you'll have to start an advertising campaign and put some money into it. But in any case, Google's own keyword research tool should not be ignored, even though they limit the data that you get for free. And finally, let's look at the keyword research tool that we have in AdWords. It's called keywords explorer and it runs on a monstrous database of 4.6 billion search queries. We use multiple sources of data to collect the database of these sites, but the main one is click stream. In short, click stream data comes from a large group of mostly free apps that you install on your computer and give them permission to collect your browsing data and minimize it and aggregate into a huge click stream database. From this database, digital marketers can then learn what people search for online and which websites they visit, but there is no personal information on any of these people. Click stream data is literally a rescue to the SEO community, as many amazing tools and features can be built on top of it. So back to its chef's keywords explorer. If I enter the same word cats, I'll get a list of almost a million search queries that contain the word cats in them. I guess when you compare this number of keyword suggestions to the number of keyword ideas in Google keyword planner, you'll see why as professionals are so might at Google, right? But what do you do with a list of almost a million search queries? Well, remember the previous lesson where we talked about keyword difficulty? I said it's a great way to narrow down a huge list of keyword ideas to those where you don't need any backlinks to rank. So let's put zero in KD filter and see what we'll get. When do cats stop growing? Can cats eat bread? Do male cats have nipples? These search queries could easily be answered with awesome blog articles. If I open the search results for can cats eat bread search query, I'll see that most of the top ranking articles get a few hundred monthly visits from search with almost no backlinks, which looks like a great opportunity to me. And that was a quick overview of how to use keyword research tools to get a ton of great keyword ideas to target on your blog. Just feed these tools with words and phrases related to your niche and examine the output. And don't forget what we've learned in lesson number three. Always look at the total search traffic potential of the top ranking pages and how many backlinks they have. And please take action before you move to the next strategy. Go play with the tools that I've just mentioned and see how many awesome article ideas you'll be able to find.

第二部分：如何使用关键词研究工具生成关键词创意。市面上有不少出色的工具，可以帮助你研究人们在 Google 上搜索的内容。下面我将介绍几款最受欢迎的关键词研究工具，并解释它们的工作原理。比如有一款名为 Answer the Public 的工具，在 SEO 和内容营销人员中相当受欢迎。也许是因为他们主页上那位极具魅力的男士吧。开个玩笑，这确实是一款非常棒的免费工具。只需输入任意词语或短语，它就会返回一份人们曾在 Google 上输入的相关问题列表。例如，如果我在搜索栏中输入“cats”，它会给出近 200 个包含“cats”的问题，比如“Can cats eat dog food”（猫能吃狗粮吗）、“Where cats like to be pet”（猫喜欢被抚摸的地方）以及“Which cats shed the least”（哪些猫掉毛最少）等等。你可能想知道，这个工具是如何知道人们在搜索什么内容的呢？其实，它的数据来源非常简单，就是 Google 的自动建议功能。当你开始输入类似“Can cats”的问题时，它会立即显示一些热门搜索；而当你继续输入下一个字母时，它会根据你输入的字母提供相应的建议。这就是这个小免费工具获取搜索查询的地方。正如其主页所述，他们使用自动建议功能以及 Google 和 Bing 来为你提供关键词建议。这里还有另一个类似的工具，它基于 Google 的自动建议功能，名为 Uber's suggest。两者之间的区别在于，Uber's suggest 并不专注于问题。而且，如你所见，所有的关键词建议都将从你的种子关键词开始。一切都以“cats”这个词开头。这两个工具都不会显示它们所建议搜索查询的月度搜索量，但你应该已经知道去哪里获取这些数据了，对吧？现在来看下一个关键词研究工具，你可能已经知道它了。它叫做 Google Keyword Planner，是 AdWords（Google 的广告平台）的一部分。这个工具在过去曾是关键词创意的最佳来源，但近年来，Google 逐渐向 SEO 专业人士隐藏了越来越多的数据。因此，如今 Google Keyword Planner 对于希望通过 Google 付费获取流量的广告商来说是个不错的工具，但对于希望从 Google 获取免费流量的 SEO 专业人士而言，就不那么友好了。然而，你仍然可以从 Google Keyword Planner 中获得很棒的关键词创意。如果我输入“cats”这个词，就能得到几百个不错的关键词创意，而且这个工具甚至还能将它们分组，这相当方便实用。主要缺点是看不到这些关键词的月度搜索量，你只能看到搜索量范围，而这并不是特别有用。如果你想查看具体数值，就必须启动一个广告活动并投入一些资金。但无论如何，即使 Google 限制了免费提供的数据量，其自家的关键词研究工具也不容忽视。最后，我们来看看 AdWords 中的关键词研究工具。它被称为 Keywords Explorer，运行在一个包含 46 亿个搜索查询的庞大数据库上。我们使用多个数据源来收集这些网站的数据库，但主要来源是点击流数据。简而言之，点击流数据来自一个庞大的免费应用群体，你将这些应用安装到电脑上，并授权它们收集你的浏览数据，然后对这些数据进行处理、汇总，最终形成一个巨大的点击流数据库。从这个数据库中，数字营销人员可以了解到人们在网络上搜索什么、访问哪些网站，但这些信息中并不包含任何个人的身份信息。点击流数据对于SEO社区来说简直就是救星，因为基于它可以构建出许多强大的工具和功能。现在回到它的“关键词探索器”。如果我输入相同的词“cats”，我会得到一个包含近一百万个含有“cats”一词的搜索查询列表。我想，当你把这个关键词建议数量与Google关键词规划器中的关键词想法数量进行比较时，你就会明白为什么专业人士如此依赖Google，对吧？但面对一个包含近一百万个搜索查询的列表，你该如何处理呢？还记得之前的课程中我们讨论过的“关键词难度”吗？我说过，这是一个将庞大的关键词想法列表缩小到那些无需任何反向链接即可排名的关键词的好方法。那么，让我们在KD筛选器中输入0，看看会得到什么结果。“猫什么时候停止生长？”“猫能吃面包吗？”“公猫有乳头吗？”这些搜索查询都可以通过精彩的博文轻松解答。如果我打开“猫能吃面包吗”这一搜索词的搜索结果，会发现大多数排名靠前的文章每月仅通过搜索获得几百次访问，几乎没有任何反向链接。在我看来，这似乎是一个绝佳的机会。以上就是如何利用关键词研究工具获取大量优质关键词创意以用于博客的简要概述。只需将这些工具输入与你的细分领域相关的词语和短语，然后分析输出结果即可。别忘了我们在第三课中学到的内容：始终关注排名靠前页面的总搜索流量潜力及其拥有的反向链接数量。在继续下一个策略之前，请务必采取行动。去使用我刚才提到的工具，看看你能发现多少出色的文章创意吧。

### 4.3 找到竞争对手排名关键词

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1q34y1r7RA?p=3)　时长 2分59秒

**原文**

Part 3, how to find keywords that your competitors are ranking for. He who wrestles with us, strengthens our nerves, and sharpens our skill. Our antagonist is our helper, Edmund Burke. This quote perfectly applies to content marketing and blogging. We can study our competitors pinpoint their best performing content and beat them at it. But first you need to know who you're up against. You might already know which websites you're competing with in Google search results, but in case you don't, just put the keywords found in the previous exercise into Google and see who ranks there. Those are your competitors. Let's try that with one of the keywords we've discovered earlier. Cancets eat dog food. When browsing Google searches out for the keyword, I see a cool website that catches my attention. The website name is way too awesome to ignore it. LoveMeow.com. I love it. So we know that loveMeow.com website ranks well for cancets eat dog food search query. Wouldn't it be awesome to know all other search queries that it ranks for? Because we have a tool for that in HREFs. Let's put loveMeow.com into HREFs and go to a report called organic keywords. Here I can see all the search queries that the target website or article ranks for. That is very cool, but not very actionable to be honest. That's why I prefer the report called top pages. It groups all these keywords that the website is ranking for by the ranking page. So I can easily see which articles bring the most search traffic to this blog. The top performing article at lovemeow.com blog is titled why it does my cat stick his tongue out. And it brings almost 900 visitors from the United States alone and the total search traffic is 1200. So how cool is that? Usually when people see this feature for the first time, they are totally blown away by the amount of value they can get from it. In just a few seconds, you can see all the best performing pages of any competitor. And there's also a column with a number of referring domains to each of these pages. So all you need to do from here is cherry pick the pages that bring them the most traffic, while having the least amount of websites linking to them. And once you're done with the first competitor, you can click on competing domains report and see which other websites are ranking for the same search queries as this competitor. Here's the cool one I see right away, catster.com, let's browse their top pages. Looks like their articles get vastly more search traffic than what we've seen at lovemeow.com. And some of them have less than 10 referring domains, which means they should be fairly easy to train. And once you're done with catster.com, you can click competing domains again and dive even deeper into your competitive space. I'm sure you get the idea. So hit pause and take action, spend some time digging into websites of your competitors and noting their best pages in the US spreadsheet. And they have one last source of awesome content by DS left for you.

**译文**

第3部分：如何找到竞争对手正在排名的关键词。与我们对峙者，能增强我们的意志，磨砺我们的技能。我们的对手就是我们的助力者——埃德蒙·伯克。这句名言在内容营销和博客写作中同样适用。我们可以研究竞争对手，精准定位他们表现最佳的内容，并在这些内容上超越他们。但首先，你需要知道你的对手是谁。你可能已经知道在Google搜索结果中哪些网站是你的竞争对手，但如果你不确定，只需将上一练习中找到的关键词输入Google，看看哪些网站出现在搜索结果中，那些就是你的竞争对手。让我们用之前发现的一个关键词来试试：“Cancets吃狗粮”。当我在Google上搜索这个关键词时，我发现了一个吸引我注意的酷炫网站。这个网站的名字实在太棒了，不容忽视——LoveMeow.com。我超爱它。因此我们知道，LoveMeow.com网站在“Cancets吃狗粮”这个搜索词上排名不错。要是能知道它还排名哪些其他搜索词，那岂不是很棒？因为我们在HREFs中就有这样的工具。让我们把LoveMeow.com输入HREFs，然后进入名为“organic keywords”（自然关键词）的报告。在这里，我可以看到目标网站或文章所排名的所有搜索查询。这非常酷，但说实话，可操作性并不强。因此，我更喜欢名为“top pages”的报告。它将网站排名所涉及的所有关键词按排名页面进行分组，这样我就能清楚地看到哪些文章为该博客带来了最多的搜索流量。在 lovemeow.com 博客上，表现最好的文章标题是《为什么我的猫会伸出舌头》。仅美国地区就为其带来了近 900 名访客，总搜索流量为 1200。这难道不酷吗？通常，当人们第一次看到这个功能时，都会被它能带来的巨大价值所震撼。只需几秒钟，你就能查看任何竞争对手表现最好的所有页面。此外，还有一个栏目会显示每个页面的引荐域名数量。因此，你只需从中挑选出那些为该网站带来最多流量、同时又有最少网站链接到的页面即可。完成对第一个竞争对手的分析后，你还可以点击“competing domains report”（竞争域名报告），查看还有哪些其他网站与该竞争对手针对相同的搜索查询进行了排名。这里有一个我一眼就看出来的好例子：catster.com，我们来浏览一下他们的热门页面。看起来他们的文章获得的搜索流量远超我们在 lovemeow.com 所见的水平。而且其中一些页面的引荐域名数量还不到 10 个，这意味着它们应该相当容易进行训练。等你完成 catster.com 的分析后，可以再次点击“竞争域名”，进一步深入挖掘你的竞争领域。我想你已经明白我的意思了。所以，现在暂停一下并立即行动起来，花些时间研究竞争对手的网站，把他们最出色的页面记录到美国表格中。此外，DS 还为你留下了最后一个优质内容来源。

### 4.4 寻找低竞争的关键词创意

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1q34y1r7RA?p=4)　时长 4分19秒

**原文**

Part four, how to find low-hanging content ideas from all over the web. I just showed you how to research the content of your competitors and find some golden nuggets that you can easily replicate on your own blog. But you can only do this one competitor at a time. How cool would it be to research the entire niche at once? Let me show you how you can do exactly that. We have a tool called Content Explorer, which is basically a database of 846 million pieces of content that each chef's crawler has collected from all around the web. And I can enter any word into the search bar and find all pieces of content that mentioned this word in their content title or both. Let's continue with the word cats as our example. I want to find all pages from all around the web that have the word cats in their title, which results in over a million pages. Now let's see how many of these pages get over a thousand pieces per month from Google. We have a filter for that here. And now we are down to slightly over 2,000 pages that generate over a thousand monthly visits from Google. That's roughly 0.1% of all pages that mentioned the word cats in their title, which says a lot about how most of the published content performs, right? But how many of these pages generate their search traffic with no backlinks point to get them? We have a filter for that too. It's called referring domains. Let's put zero into it. And now I'm down to 63 pages only. Let's start them by the amount of search traffic to see the best ones on top. And if I scroll down a bit I'll see an awesome article, the best fleet treatment for cats for 2017. It gets almost 5000 visits per month from Google while having zero backlinks, what an amazing opportunity. I can also click the details button and see which keywords bring search traffic to this article. Looks like it ranks really high for things like best fleet treatment for cats, best fleet medicine for cats, fleet treatment for cats and such. That's an amazing content opportunity that took me less than a minute to discover. Here I have a few more great articles from that list. How to call a cat in heat, tips for moving a cat long distance, save fruits for your cat. All of them get decent search traffic with zero backlinks. And I only try a single keyword, cats, and only focus on articles with zero backlinks and over 1000 visitors from search. I can also try some other relevant keywords like kittens and losing my filters a bit to get content ideas with a bit less traffic and a few more backlinks, which will give me vastly more results to explore. So how cool is that? As you can tell, content explorer tool is a unique and insanely valuable tool because it allows you to filter through almost the entire internet of content. And find what brings the most search traffic with the list amount of backlinks. When I shared this trick with our Facebook community, people were totally blown away and immediately used it to find great content ideas for their sites. So hit pause and try this yourself. I'm sure you will find something cool for your own blog too. And one last thing I have to mention is your website's domain rating, which represents the strength of the overall backlink profile of your website. As a general rule, it can be challenging to a trend websites that have high domain rating. For example, here you can see an article from Huffington Post that is titled the top 100 most adorable and cute cat names. It has zero referring domains, but the domain rating of Huffington Post.com is 80, which is very high. So if the domain rating of your own website is much smaller than that, like 60 or below, it isn't likely that your article will outtrankhuffingtonpost.com. I mean, if both articles have zero backlinks, it is obvious that Google will pick the one that was published on a stronger website. But if you build a bunch of quality backlinks to your own article, you do have a chance to outtrank even high DR domains. Please also note that the numbers that you see in Connet Explorer are cached. I mean, there's a small discrepancy in backlink and traffic numbers for the same article inside Explorer and Connet Explorer, but that discrepancy shouldn't be too big as we update all numbers quite regularly. And this wraps up the first strategies that will supply you with an almost never-ending source of great content ideas. And now it's time to prioritize these ideas in your content calendar.

**译文**

第四部分：如何从全网挖掘现成的内容创意。我刚才向你展示了如何研究竞争对手的内容，从中发现一些你完全可以在自家博客上轻松复制的“黄金机会”。但这种方法一次只能研究一个竞争对手。要是能一次性研究整个细分领域，那该多酷？让我来演示一下具体操作方法。我们有一个名为 Content Explorer 的工具，它本质上是一个包含 8.46 亿篇内容的数据库，这些内容均由 Chef 的爬虫从全网各处采集而来。我可以在搜索栏中输入任意关键词，找到所有在内容标题中提及该关键词（或同时出现在标题和内容中）的文章。我们以“cats”一词继续举例。我想找到全网所有标题中包含“cats”一词的页面，搜索结果超过一百万页。现在，我们再筛选出这些页面中哪些每月能从谷歌获得超过一千次访问。这里有一个对应的筛选条件。现在，符合条件的页面已减少到略多于 2000 页，这些页面每月能从谷歌带来超过一千次访问。这大约占所有在标题中提及“cats”一词的页面的0.1%，这充分说明了大多数已发布内容的表现情况，对吧？但这些页面中，有多少是通过没有任何反向链接来获取搜索流量的呢？我们也有一个用于筛选这个条件的过滤器，它被称为“referring domains”（引荐域名）。现在我们将它设置为“零”。现在我只剩下63个页面了。让我们按搜索流量从高到低排序，看看排名靠前的页面有哪些。如果我向下滚动一点，会发现一篇非常棒的文章——《2017年最佳猫咪福来恩驱虫方案》。这篇文章每月从Google获得近5000次访问，却没有任何反向链接，这真是个绝佳的机会。我还可以点击“详情”按钮，查看哪些关键词为这篇文章带来了搜索流量。看起来它在“最佳猫咪福来恩驱虫方案”、“最佳猫咪福来恩药物”、“猫咪福来恩驱虫”等关键词上排名都非常高。这是一个绝佳的创作机会，而我发现它只用了不到一分钟的时间。在这里，我还有列表中其他几篇很棒的文章，比如《如何呼唤发情期的猫咪》、《长途搬家时照顾猫咪的技巧》以及《适合猫咪食用的水果》。它们都获得了不错的搜索流量，却没有任何反向链接。我只尝试了一个关键词“cats”，并且只关注那些没有反向链接、但来自搜索的访问量超过1000次的文章。我还可以尝试一些其他相关关键词，比如“kittens”，并稍微放宽筛选条件，以获取流量稍低但反向链接稍多的内容创意，这将为我提供大量可供探索的结果。这有多酷呢？正如你所看到的，内容探索工具是一款独特且极其有价值的工具，因为它能让你筛选几乎整个互联网上的内容，并找出哪些内容能带来最多的搜索流量，同时所需的反向链接数量最少。当我把这个技巧分享给我们的Facebook社群时，大家都被彻底震撼了，并立即用它为自己的网站找到了绝佳的内容创意。所以，不妨暂停一下，亲自试试看吧。我相信你也能为自己的博客找到一些有趣的内容。最后还有一点必须提到的是你网站的域名评级（domain rating），它代表了你网站整体反向链接档案的强度。通常来说，对于拥有高域名评级的网站而言，追赶趋势可能会比较有挑战性。例如，在这里你可以看到一篇来自Huffington Post的文章，标题是“100个最可爱、最萌的猫咪名字”。Huffington Post.com 的引用域名数量为 0，但其域名评级（Domain Rating）高达 80，属于非常高的水平。因此，如果你自己网站的域名评级远低于此（例如 60 或更低），你的文章几乎不可能在排名上超越 Huffington Post。我的意思是，如果两篇文章都没有反向链接，那么很明显，Google 会选择发布在更强网站上的那篇文章。但如果你为自己的文章建立大量高质量的反向链接，你就有机会超越那些高域名评级的网站。另外请注意，你在 Connet Explorer 中看到的数据是缓存的。也就是说，Explorer 和 Connet Explorer 中对同一篇文章的反向链接和流量数据可能存在轻微差异，但这种差异应该不会太大，因为我们会定期更新所有数据。以上就是第一组策略，它们将为你提供一个几乎取之不尽的优质内容创意来源。现在，是时候将这些创意优先安排到你的内容日历中了。

### 4.5 确定内容创意的优先次序

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1q34y1r7RA?p=5)　时长 5分1秒

**原文**

Part 5. How to prioritize your list of content ideas. If you were taking action while watching this lesson, I'm sure your spreadsheet of content ideas is full of golden nuggets, which should feel quite satisfying already. But how do you decide which of them you should tackle first? Well, in the early lessons of this course, a promise that we're going to focus on the business value of blogging. What's the point of investing your time and resources to bring people to your website if these people won't buy from you? So we need to add one more column, to our spreadsheet and name it business value. To fill a cell in this column, you'll need to ask yourself, what are the chances that a person looking for the thing in Google and reading my article on the topic would become my customer? I usually give my content ideas a score from 0 to 3, where 3 means that my product of service is an absolutely irreplaceable solution for a target search query, which leads to an easy cell. Two means that my business will help people with what they're searching for, but it's not essential in solving their search query. One means that my business can only be mentioned in passing, because the search query is only slightly related to what I sell. And zero means that there's not even a chance to screen a single mention of my business into the article. Early in this course, I told you that with each other's blog, we never had a goal to grow monthly amount of traffic. We wanted to make our blog a customer acquisition channel and grow the monthly number of leads that it generates. That's why we often had to ignore topics with huge traffic potential, in favor of topics with huge business potential. Let me explain that with a very vivid example. Most likely you've heard about HubSpot. They are one of the biggest brands in digital marketing software field. They have 1800 employees and a huge content marketing team that runs their blog. According to HREF's HubSpot blog gets almost 2 million visits per month from Google, which is an absolutely staggering number. So let's look at their best performing articles using the top pages reported in HREF's. And here's their best article. It brings them almost 80,000 visitors per month in the U.S. alone, and accounts for 8% of their entire blog traffic. How to make an animated gift in Photoshop? Yeah, it's not a mistake. Their best performing article is a tutorial on making a gift image. Does this article grow their business? Well, the product that HubSpot sells can be described as a marketing CRM. And if you ask me, the role of someone looking for a tutorial on making a gift image to becoming a paid user of a marketing CRM is kind of amazing. I mean, people who are searching Google for how to make a gift are clearly not their dream customers. So this content idea would get a business potential score of zero from me. Now let's examine our own blog and see if you will practice what we preach. Here are the articles that bring the most search traffic to HREF's blog. Keyword Research, top Google searches, website traffic, SEO tips, SEO audit, etc. Obviously, the articles are nowhere near how to make a gift guide in terms of traffic numbers. But they convert readers into customers of HREF's very well. Here's the tweet from last week to prove it. This guide took HREF's retrial after reading our guide to long-tail keywords, and a few weeks later, he tweeted that he became our paid customer. This is exactly what we're looking to achieve with almost every article that we publish on our blog. And it is utterly important to keep this in mind at all times. Search volume doesn't equal business opportunity. The searchers intend behind you search query does. That is why marketers are so obsessed with keywords that have the word buy or its synonyms in them. Best place to buy DSLR camera, hire a business coach, rent an apartment in London. These searches clearly indicate that the person behind them is one step away from pulling money from their wallet. But in all honesty, you should not ignore their generic versions with much higher search volume just because the search traffic coming from them would be harder to convert into customers. Especially if you have some experience writing good sales copy and persuading people to buy what you sell. A person searching for business coach might not be ready to hire one right away, but it is your article that could persuade them to do so. So if the more specific search query with a clear buying intent gets a business value of three, the generic one would still get two from me. All you need to do is just stay reasonable with your business values course and don't fall for writing guides on how to create a gift image unless, of course, you sell some kind of software for creating gifts. And that's the end of lesson number four. Now you should have a perfect understanding of where to get almost unlimited content ideas and how to read them for search traffic potential, ranking difficulty, and business value. And in the next lesson, I'll show you how to make sure that your article is properly optimized for the search query that you're targeting. Sounds good, then I'll see you in lesson number five.

**译文**

第5部分：如何为你的内容创意列表确定优先级。如果你在观看本课时已经付诸行动，那么你的内容创意电子表格中一定已经填满了许多“金点子”，这本身应该已经让你很有成就感了。但问题是，你该如何决定先处理哪一个呢？在本课程的早期课程中，我们曾承诺将重点探讨博客对业务的价值。如果你投入时间和资源吸引访客来到网站，而他们最终并不会从你这里购买，那这一切又有什么意义呢？因此，我们需要在电子表格中再添加一列，并将其命名为“业务价值”。要填写这一列中的某个单元格，你需要问自己：一个在谷歌上搜索相关内容并阅读我的文章的人，成为我客户的概率有多大？我通常会给自己的内容创意打0到3分，其中3分表示我的产品或服务对于目标搜索词而言是绝对不可替代的解决方案，这很容易促成转化；2分则表示我的业务能帮助人们解决他们正在搜索的问题，但并非解决该问题的关键所在。这意味着我的业务只能被顺带提及，因为搜索查询与我销售的内容仅略有相关。而零则表示，甚至没有可能从文章中筛选出哪怕一条关于我业务的提及。在本课程的早期，我曾告诉过大家，对于彼此的博客，我们从未设定过提升月度访问量的目标。我们希望将博客打造成一个客户获取渠道，并提升其每月产生的潜在客户数量。因此，我们常常不得不放弃那些具有巨大流量潜力的话题，转而选择那些具有巨大商业潜力的话题。让我用一个非常生动的例子来解释这一点。你很可能听说过 HubSpot。他们是数字营销软件领域最大的品牌之一，拥有 1800 名员工，并有一支庞大的内容营销团队负责运营其博客。根据 HREF 的数据，HubSpot 博客每月从 Google 获得的访问量接近 200 万次，这是一个绝对惊人的数字。那么，让我们借助 HREF 报告中的热门页面，来看看他们表现最好的文章。这是他们表现最好的一篇文章，仅在美国每月就为他们带来了近 8 万名访客，占其整个博客流量的 8%。如何在 Photoshop 中制作一个动画礼物？是的，你没看错。他们表现最好的文章是一篇关于如何制作礼物图片的教程。这篇文章能推动他们的业务增长吗？嗯，HubSpot 销售的产品可以描述为营销 CRM。而在我看来，一个搜索礼物图片教程的人最终成为营销 CRM 付费用户的转变过程，简直令人惊叹。我的意思是，那些在谷歌上搜索“如何制作礼物”的人，显然不是他们的理想客户。因此，就商业潜力而言，这个内容创意在我这里只能得零分。现在，让我们审视一下我们自己的博客，看看我们是否践行了自己所倡导的理念。以下是为 HREF 博客带来最多搜索流量的文章：关键词研究、谷歌热门搜索、网站流量、SEO 技巧、SEO 审计等。显然，这些文章的流量数据与“如何制作礼物”指南相差甚远。但它们却能非常有效地将读者转化为 HREF 的客户。上周的这条推文就是最好的证明：这位读者在阅读了我们关于长尾关键词的指南后，对 HREF 产生了兴趣；几周后，他发推文称自己已成为我们的付费客户。这正是我们希望在博客上发布的几乎每一篇文章中所实现的目标。这一点必须时刻牢记。搜索量并不等同于商业机会，真正重要的是搜索者背后的意图。这就是为什么营销人员如此热衷于那些包含“购买”或其同义词的关键词。例如：“购买数码单反相机的最佳地点”、“聘请一位商业教练”、“在伦敦租一套公寓”。这些搜索清楚地表明，搜索者距离掏出钱包只差一步之遥。但说实话，你也不应忽视那些搜索量更高的通用版本，尽管从这些关键词而来的搜索流量更难转化为客户。尤其是当你有撰写优质销售文案并说服他人购买你所售产品的经验时。一个搜索“商业教练”的人可能尚未准备好立即聘请一位，但你的文章或许能说服他们采取行动。因此，如果一个具有明确购买意图的更具体搜索查询的商业价值为3分，那么通用版本的搜索查询在我这里也能获得2分。你只需在商业价值课程中保持理性即可，不要轻信那些关于如何创作礼品图片的写作指南——当然，除非你确实销售某种用于创作礼品的软件。以上就是第四课的结束内容。现在，你应该已经完全掌握了如何获取几乎无限的内容创意，以及如何评估这些创意的搜索流量潜力、排名难度和商业价值。在下一课中，我将向你展示如何确保你的文章针对目标搜索查询进行了妥善优化。听起来不错，那我们第五课再见。

### 5.1 什么是搜索意图，为什么它很重要？

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1q34y1r7RA?p=6)　时长 6分0秒

**原文**

Welcome to the lesson number five, how to optimize your article for a target keyword and generate maximum search traffic with it. Here's what we are about to cover. The important searcher intent and how to match it with your article, how to optimize your article for a given keyword, so that to make it perfectly relevant in the eyes of Google, how to squeeze maximum search traffic out of your article, and finally, how to audit your existing content and get the most out of it. So let's go. Part one, what is searcher intent and how to match it? In lesson number three, I said that the biggest mistake that keeps bloggers away from getting search traffic is writing articles about things that no one is searching for. Well, as soon as newbie bloggers learn about the keyword research and how to identify the total search traffic potential of a certain topic, which we have covered in lesson number three, they start making their second biggest mistake, targeting the wrong keyword. How is that even possible you may ask? Well, let me share a quick story with you. Once upon a time, we received a guest post pitch from one of our customers. He wanted to write a post on how to use a chef's for advertising research. We have this page search section in a chef's site explorer tool, which shows you the keywords that the target website is advertising for via Google Adwords, along with their ads and lending pages with a send this paid traffic. And this is what he was offering to cover in his guest article, along with some nice examples. I thought it would be nice to have a post on a chef's blog featuring this functionality. So if we're worried this email to my team, and that's to do a quick keyword research, and find a good keyword that we could possibly target with that guest article. And they could back to me with the keyword advertising research, which has the monthly search volume of 450 searches per month in the US, and around 100 visits from search to some of the top ranking results. So that's a green light, right? Wrong. By looking at the top 10 ranking pages for this keyword, I can see that they talk about advertising research in general. While the guest article that we were suggested was going to talk about researching the adwords paper, click strategies of your competitors, which is a very specific and narrow topic. I mean, people who are searching in Google for advertising research are not necessarily interested in paper, click advertising specifically. Otherwise, they would Google for paper, click advertising research, right? So we would make a big mistake if we tried to target the keyword advertising research with a article about paper, click advertising research. That article would not meet the so-called social intent, which Google can easily figure out by looking at how users behave after clicking on your article in the search results. They would simply bounce if they won't see what they were looking for. So which search query would be the right one for the article? I brainstormed a few relevant searches, tested my assumptions in keywords explorer, and got this. Adwords competitor analysis, paper, click competitor analysis, paper, click competitor. Sure, the search volume on all three of them is not particularly exciting, but at least they are perfectly relevant to this suggested guest post. And the top-ranking pages for these keywords do get a little bit of traffic from search, according to each chefs, which I'm reminding you is always an underestimation. And fortunately, we didn't end up publishing the article, so I can't show you the results, but at least they have a cool story that illustrates the importance of targeting the right searcher intent. Way to often you be bloggers would try to optimize their article for the keyword with the highest search volume, ignoring the fact that their article doesn't really match the intent of the people who are searching for the keyword. And while they think they're making a smart decisions, they're actually shooting themselves in the food, because Google is pretty smart at figuring out what people want when they search for something. And if your page doesn't give them that, it won't break at the top. So if you have a great article idea, but there's not much search demand for the topic, don't try to treat Google and optimize it for something it is not, a broad topic with high search demand. You should pick the most relevant search query even if the search volume seems low. At least this way you get a good chance of actually ranking for that keyword and getting some highly relevant search traffic. Here's a small exercise that will help you to reconform the keyword. Take the draft of your article and send it to a few friends or co-workers, asking them what it is about. The words that they will use to describe your article will help you understand what kind of search queries it deserves to rank foreign Google. But what if you don't have an article yet? What if you just discovered the cool keyword and you want to write an article on that topic and make sure it would perfectly match the searcher intent? In this case the best thing you could do is put yourself in the shoes of people who would search for that keyword in Google and ask yourself a few simple questions. What are people looking for when they search for that keyword? What goal do they have in mind? What is their motivation? What kind of search result would perfectly satisfy them? In other words, think of the searcher intent behind that keyword. For many keywords it is plain obvious. Like how to tie a tie. Clearly people need an instruction on how to do that. Preferably visual because it's almost impossible to explain that with words. But sometimes the searcher intent can be quite ambiguous. Like the keyword violas. It can be a music instrument or a flower. And if you're unsure about the right searcher intent for some query, the best thing you can do is put it into Google and see what comes up. Google has invested years of work and billions of dollars into figuring out what people mean when they type something into the search bar. And they are actually quite good at it. For how to tie a tie, they suggest a short video clip. Because their data in the user behavior signals showed that this is what the best majority of searches want. And for violas, Google gives a mix of searches out. Some about the musical instrument and some about the flower. This means that the searcher intent for that keyword is actually to fold. And there is nothing you could do about it. So that is searcher intent in a nutshell. It really boils down to putting yourself in the shoes of searchers and using a logic to understand what would satisfy them. Make sure you always check the search results for the keyword that you decide to target. If the pages that rank there are entirely different from what you were going to say, you probably misunderstood the searcher intent behind that keyword. And once you choose the right keyword for your article, here's how you optimize for it.

**译文**

欢迎来到第五课：如何针对目标关键词优化文章，并借此产生最大的搜索流量。我们将涵盖以下内容：重要的搜索者意图以及如何通过文章与之匹配；如何针对特定关键词优化文章，使其在 Google 眼中具有高度相关性；如何从文章中榨取最大的搜索流量；最后，如何审核现有内容并使其发挥最大价值。那么，我们开始吧。第一部分：什么是搜索者意图？如何与之匹配？在第三课中，我曾提到，阻碍博主获得搜索流量的最大错误是撰写一些根本没人搜索的内容。然而，一旦新手博主了解了关键词研究以及如何评估某个主题的总搜索流量潜力（我们在第三课中已经讲过），他们就会开始犯下第二大错误——针对错误的关键词。你可能会问，这怎么可能呢？让我给你讲一个简短的故事。很久以前，我们收到了一位客户发来的客座文章投稿邀请。他想写一篇关于如何使用 Chef 进行广告研究的帖子。在 Chef 的网站探索工具中，有一个页面搜索部分，它会显示目标网站通过 Google Adwords 投放广告所使用的关键词，以及相关的广告和落地页，这些页面都在引导付费流量。他原本打算在客座文章中涵盖这些内容，并附上一些不错的示例。我觉得在 Chef 的博客上发布一篇介绍这一功能的帖子会很不错。因此，我们担心需要给我的团队发送邮件，让他们快速做一次关键词研究，找出一个可能适合这篇客座文章的关键词。他们反馈说，这个关键词的广告研究每月在美国有 450 次搜索量，并且能带来大约 100 次来自搜索的访问，其中一些还进入了排名靠前的结果。这看起来是个绿灯信号，对吧？错。通过查看该关键词排名前十的页面，我发现它们讨论的都是广告研究的一般性内容。虽然我们被建议的客座文章原本打算讨论 AdWords 论文研究以及竞争对手的点击策略，但这个主题非常具体且狭窄。我的意思是，那些在谷歌上搜索“广告研究”的人，未必对“论文点击广告”特别感兴趣。否则，他们就会直接搜索“论文点击广告研究”，对吧？因此，如果我们试图用一篇关于“论文点击广告研究”的文章来定位“广告研究”这个关键词，那将是一个巨大的错误。这篇文章将无法满足所谓的“用户意图”，而谷歌可以通过观察用户在点击文章后的行为轻松判断这一点。如果用户找不到他们想要的内容，他们就会直接跳出页面。那么，哪个搜索词才适合这篇文章呢？我 brainstorm 了一些相关的搜索词，并在关键词分析工具中验证了我的假设，结果如下：AdWords 竞争对手分析、论文点击竞争对手分析、论文点击竞争对手。当然，这三个词的搜索量都不算特别高，但至少它们与这篇建议的客座文章完全相关。根据每个厨师的说法，这些关键词排名靠前的页面确实能从搜索中获得一些流量——我在此提醒各位，这通常还是一种低估。幸运的是，我们最终没有发布那篇文章，因此我无法向大家展示结果，但至少它有一个精彩的故事，说明了定位正确搜索意图的重要性。博主们常常试图针对搜索量最高的关键词优化文章，却忽略了他们的文章其实并不符合搜索该关键词人群的真实意图。虽然他们以为自己做出了明智的决定，但实际上是在自断后路，因为谷歌非常擅长判断用户在搜索时真正想要什么。如果你的页面无法满足他们的需求，就无法在搜索结果中占据前列。因此，如果你有一个很棒的文章创意，但该主题的搜索需求并不高，就不要试图欺骗谷歌，将其优化成一个与内容无关、搜索需求广泛的话题。你应该选择最相关的搜索查询，即使它的搜索量看起来很低。至少这样，你就有很大机会真正针对该关键词获得排名，并带来一些高度相关的搜索流量。这里有一个小练习，可以帮助你重新确认关键词。拿出你的文章草稿，把它发给几个朋友或同事，请他们说说这篇文章讲的是什么。他们用来描述你文章的词语，将有助于你理解这篇文章应该针对哪些搜索查询在 Google 上获得排名。但如果你还没有文章呢？如果你只是发现了一个很棒的关键词，并想围绕该主题写一篇文章，同时确保它能完美匹配搜索者意图，该怎么办？在这种情况下，最好的做法就是把自己放在那些会在 Google 上搜索该关键词的人的角度，问自己几个简单的问题：人们在搜索该关键词时到底在寻找什么？他们心中有怎样的目标？他们的动机是什么？什么样的搜索结果能让他们完全满意？换句话说，思考该关键词背后的搜索者意图。对于许多关键词来说，这一点显而易见。比如“如何系领带”。显然，人们需要相关的操作指导。最好是使用视觉方式，因为几乎不可能用语言来解释清楚。但有时搜索意图可能相当模糊。比如关键词“violas”，它既可以指一种乐器，也可以指一种花。如果你对某个查询的正确搜索意图不确定，最好的办法就是把它输入到 Google 中，看看会出现什么结果。Google 投入了多年的工作和数十亿美元，致力于弄清楚当人们在搜索栏中输入内容时到底想要什么。而他们在这一方面确实做得相当出色。对于“如何打领带”，他们会推荐一段短视频。因为用户行为数据信号表明，这是大多数搜索者想要的。而对于“violas”，Google 会混合呈现搜索结果，有些是关于乐器的，有些是关于花的。这意味着该关键词的搜索意图实际上是“混合的”。对此你无能为力。这就是搜索意图的简要说明。归根结底，就是设身处地为搜索者着想，并用逻辑去理解什么能让他们满意。务必始终检查你决定要针对的关键词的搜索结果。如果在该页面排名中的内容与你原本想要表达的内容完全不同，那么你很可能误解了该关键词背后的搜索意图。一旦你为自己的文章选定了正确的关键词，接下来就是如何针对它进行优化。

### 5.2 如何为目标关键词优化内容

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1q34y1r7RA?p=7)　时长 4分32秒

**原文**

Part 2. How to optimize your article for a given keyword. So you have a great keyword in mind and you're absolutely sure that you're going to match the searcher intent with the article that you're about to write. How to optimize your article for that keyword? Well, you don't. I mean, if your article perfectly matches the searcher intent for that keyword and gives people exactly what they're looking for, that is nearly 80% of all optimization work. Yes, Google is just a machine and it is not smart enough to actually read your article like a human being and understand why it is better than thousands of other articles on the same topic. But Google isn't dump either. Squeezing your target keyword into your article more frequently than your competitors was a good way to trick Google 10 years ago, but not so much today. Let's examine the searchers out for the keyword how to learn SEO. How are article ranks at position number 5? If we count the features snippet and the people also ask box. How many times do you think we use the phrase how to learn SEO in the article? 1. Only in the headline. So maybe that's why we don't drink number 1 for how to learn SEO's search query. Maybe the competing pages use this keyword more frequently in their content. Let's see. And no. Both articles that rank above us have zero mentions of that keyword. So why do they rank so high then? That's because Google has enough technology and enough ranking signals to be able to find the most relevant page for a certain search query without relying on how many times that keyword was used on the page. In fact, we have studied the top 10 ranking pages for 2 million random search queries and what we discovered was quite fun. 75% of all pages the ranked in Google Top 10 don't have even a single mention of the search query that they rank for any way on the page. Back to our example with how to learn SEO's search query. Google is smart enough to understand that phrases like SEO Learning Center or online resources for learning SEO are perfectly relevant to what the user is searching for, even though they don't match the search query word for word. This is why I suggested that by nailing the searcher intent with your content, you automatically do 80% of all optimization work. Your article is likely to have enough synonyms and relevant words and phrases for Google to be able to understand what it is about. So does that mean you should ignore any kind of keyword optimization and write as you please? Not really. Google's thumb is to use your target keyword or at least the most important words of your keyword in the following places. URL, title, headline and content. Let's go back to the search results for how to learn SEO. The main words of the keyword are obviously learn and SEO. All of the top 10 ranking pages have the word SEO in their URLs and titles. As for the word learn, some of the pages have it in their URLs and titles, and others get away with the synonyms and closely related words like resources, tutorial, training, teach. So as you can tell, Google allows you quite some flexibility in keyword targeting. That is why by simply matching the searcher intent with the content of your article, you're already making it 80% relevant to the search query, because you are already using all the right words. I'd say that further finding your URL title, headline and content will probably move the needle by another 10%. This percentage is quite arbitrary, there's zero signs behind it, but you get the idea. And I'm sure you want to learn about the final 10%. There's a great article by Cyrus Shepherd where he explains rather advanced things like TFIDF, which stands for term frequency, inverse document frequency, semantic distance and term relationships, core currents and phrase-based indexing, entity salience, etc. There's also a great article by Dr. Pete the Chines a bit of light on how Google reads and understands your search queries in order to find the most relevant search result. Both of these posts are absolutely great reads and they highly recommend you to check them out if you're serious about SEO, but at the same time they're just scratching the surface of how Google really works. Their algorithms are much more complicated. So unless you're a professional SEO, I don't recommend you to get carried away with advanced on-page SEO tactics, which are quite technical. The foundation of your success in search is the actual content, not the SEO enhancements that you apply to it. To win in the search results today, you need to be an authority and expert on the topics on which you're writing. And if your article is objectively better than what all are the ranks in Google Top 10, you should be there soon.

**译文**

第二部分：如何针对特定关键词优化文章。假设你已经有了一个很棒的关键词，并且完全确定即将撰写的文章能够满足搜索者的意图。那么，该如何针对该关键词优化文章呢？其实，你并不需要刻意优化。我的意思是，如果你的文章能够完美匹配该关键词的搜索意图，并为用户提供他们真正需要的内容，这几乎就完成了80%的优化工作。是的，Google只是一台机器，它并不像人类那样智能，无法真正阅读你的文章并理解为什么它比成千上万篇同类主题的文章更出色。但Google也并不傻。10年前，通过在文章中比竞争对手更频繁地插入目标关键词是一种欺骗Google的有效方法，但如今已不再奏效。让我们以“如何学习SEO”这一关键词为例，看看搜索结果的情况。排名第五的文章表现如何？如果我们将特色摘要（featured snippet）和“人们还问”（people also ask）板块都计算在内，你觉得文章中出现了多少次“如何学习SEO”这个短语呢？1. 只在标题中出现。也许这就是为什么我们无法在“如何学习SEO”的搜索结果中排名第一的原因。也许竞争对手的页面在内容中更频繁地使用这个关键词。我们来看看。答案是否定的。排名在我们之上的两篇文章中都没有提到这个关键词。那么，为什么它们的排名却这么高呢？这是因为谷歌拥有足够强大的技术和排名信号，能够找到与特定搜索查询最相关的页面，而无需依赖关键词在页面中出现的次数。事实上，我们研究了200万个随机搜索查询中排名前10的页面，结果相当有趣：75%的谷歌排名前10页面中，甚至完全没有提及它们所排名对应的搜索查询。回到我们关于“如何学习SEO”的搜索查询示例。谷歌足够智能，能够理解像“SEO学习中心”或“在线SEO学习资源”这样的短语与用户搜索内容完全相关，即使它们与搜索查询并非逐字匹配。这就是为什么我建议，只要你的内容准确把握了搜索意图，你就自动完成了80%的优化工作。您的文章很可能包含足够的同义词和相关词语及短语，让 Google 能够理解其内容。那么，这是否意味着您可以忽略任何关键词优化，想怎么写就怎么写呢？其实并非如此。Google 的建议是，在以下位置使用您的目标关键词，或者至少使用关键词中最重要的词语：URL、标题、标题和内容。让我们回到“如何学习 SEO”的搜索结果。该关键词的核心词语显然是“学习”和“SEO”。所有排名前十的页面都在其 URL 和标题中包含了“SEO”一词。至于“学习”一词，有些页面在 URL 和标题中使用了它，而另一些页面则通过使用同义词和密切相关的词语（如资源、教程、培训、教学）来达到效果。因此，您可以看出，Google 在关键词定位方面给予了相当大的灵活性。这就是为什么只需将搜索者的意图与文章内容相匹配，就能让您的文章与搜索查询的相关性达到 80%——因为您已经使用了所有正确的词语。我认为，进一步优化 URL、标题、标题和内容，可能还会再提升 10% 的效果。这个百分比相当随意，它后面没有任何依据，但你明白我的意思。而且我敢肯定，你肯定想了解最后那10%的内容。Cyrus Shepherd有一篇很棒的文章，他在这篇文章中解释了一些相当高级的概念，比如TFIDF（词频-逆文档频率）、语义距离和术语关系、核心流和基于短语的索引、实体显著性等。此外，Dr. Pete（The Chines）也有一篇精彩的文章，它揭示了一些关于谷歌如何读取和理解你的搜索查询以找到最相关搜索结果的信息。这两篇文章都非常值得一读，如果你认真对待SEO，我强烈建议你看看。但与此同时，它们也只是触及了谷歌运作机制的表面。谷歌的算法要复杂得多。因此，除非你是专业的SEO人员，否则我不建议你沉迷于那些技术含量较高的高级页面SEO策略。你在搜索中取得成功的基础是实际内容本身，而不是你附加在其上的SEO优化手段。如今，要想在搜索结果中脱颖而出，你需要成为自己所写主题领域的权威和专家。如果你的文章客观上比谷歌搜索结果前十名中的所有内容都要优秀，那么你很快就能跻身其中。

### 5.3 如何优化内容以获得更多关键词排名

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1q34y1r7RA?p=8)　时长 3分59秒

**原文**

3. How to squeeze maximum search traffic out of your article. What we've just covered is how to optimize your article for a single keyword. But as you already know, it is rare that the page was drinking Google for a single keyword only. I mean, you can't drink number one for how to die a tie, and not drink anywhere in top 10 for something like how to die a tie tutorial. Take any search query and there would be hundreds, sometimes thousands of other ways to put it. And Google tends to rank pretty much the same pages for all these relevant search queries. And here's the best part. Your page might be ranking poorly for that main keyword, but still get decent search traffic from all these variations. Let's examine the search results for the query, website traffic using our keywords explore tool. As you can see, our article at HREF's blog only ranks number five. But it still gets almost 6,000 visits from search according to HREF's. All because it ranks for over 600 other relevant keywords, and that's in the U.S. alone. Let's look at the global search traffic data via site explorer tool. So the total traffic to this page is almost 9,000 visits per month, where 63% is coming from the United States, and 10% from the United Kingdom. Pretty decent result for an article that doesn't rank in top three for its main keyword right. So let's look at the keywords that are bringing it traffic from Google. Let's say traffic, check website traffic, website stats, website traffic estimator, website popularity, how to see how much traffic a website gets. That's a pretty wide range of search queries that it ranks for. I mean, things like website popularity and website traffic estimator are not so closely related, but Google decided that our article is worth ranking for both of them. Oh, and should I say that our article doesn't mention any of these two search queries? That's because we are practicing what we preach and focus on creating great in-depth authority content rather than feeling it with hundreds of keywords. But who doesn't want more traffic, right? So here's how we are going to optimize this article for maximum search traffic. Number one, we need to study the keywords that we already rank for and make sure that we mention them in our article. Like I just showed you our article ranks for search queries like website popularity and website traffic estimator that are not even present anywhere in our article. But don't get me wrong. We are not going to stuff our article with every keyword variation that we want to rank for. We are going to think of a natural way to rewrite our article, mentioning these things. We mostly looking for keywords that could be turned into a few additional paragraphs in our article. And website popularity seems like a great topic that we could expand on. Second, we need to study the keywords that competing pages rank for, but our page doesn't. To do that, I'm going to use our amazing content gap tool that can be found within site explorer. So we'll start with the keywords that two pages above us rank for, but our page doesn't. And here are a few search queries that seem promising. website traffic report, track website traffic, major website traffic. These things easily deserve a few paragraphs in our article that will add value to anyone reading it. And finally, three, we need to study other popular searches relevant to our main search query. For the purpose, we have quite a few reports and keywords explorer under the keyword ADS section. Also use Google keyword planner tool, which will suggest quite a few interesting keywords to consider. All in all, the goal here is to study the things that people are searching for, related to your main topic, and try to expand your article based on that knowledge. In fact, this is how you should be writing your articles in the first place. Study what people are searching for and create an outline for your article based on these searches. This will help your future article to rank for all these searches and get maximum search traffic from Google. This is probably the best advice that I can give you in terms of optimizing your article for Google. Knowing what people search for around the specific topic will help you create an amazingly useful article for these people. And here's where you should start applying everything that you've just learned from this lesson.

**译文**

3. 如何从文章中榨取最大的搜索流量。我们刚才讲的是如何针对单个关键词优化文章。但正如你所知，一个页面通常不会只为单个关键词带来流量。我的意思是，你不可能在“如何系领带”上排名第一，却在“如何系领带教程”这样的关键词上连前十都进不去。随便找一个搜索查询，就会有成百上千种其他的表达方式。而谷歌往往会为所有这些相关的搜索查询排名差不多的页面。最棒的是，你的页面可能在该主关键词上的排名并不理想，但仍能通过这些变体关键词获得可观的搜索流量。让我们用关键词探索工具来研究一下“网站流量”这个查询的搜索结果。如你所见，我们HREF博客上的那篇文章只排在第五位。但根据HREF的数据，它仍然能带来近6,000次搜索访问。这都因为它还针对另外600多个相关关键词有排名，而这还只是在美国的数据。我们再通过网站探索工具看看全球搜索流量的数据。所以，这个页面的总流量几乎达到了每月9,000次访问，其中63%来自美国，10%来自英国。对于一篇在主要关键词排名中未能进入前三名的文章来说，这已经算是相当不错的结果了。那么，我们来看看是哪些关键词为它带来了来自Google的流量吧。比如“traffic”、“check website traffic”、“website stats”、“website traffic estimator”、“website popularity”、“how to see how much traffic a website gets”等等。这些搜索查询的范围相当广泛。我的意思是，像“website popularity”和“website traffic estimator”这样的词其实关联度并不高，但Google认为我们的文章值得为这两个词都排名。哦，顺便说一句，我们的文章其实并没有提到这两个搜索查询中的任何一个。这是因为我们正在践行自己所倡导的理念——专注于创作优质、深入且具有权威性的内容，而不是用成百上千个关键词去填充文章。不过，谁不想要更多流量呢？对吧？所以，接下来我们将这样优化这篇文章，以获得最大的搜索流量。第一，我们需要研究我们已经排名的关键词，并确保在文章中提及它们。就像我刚才向你展示的，我们的文章在“网站热度”和“网站流量估算器”这类搜索词上的排名表现，而这些关键词甚至在我们文章中完全没有任何提及。但别误会，我们并不会为了追求排名，就把所有想覆盖的关键词变体都塞进文章里。我们会思考一种自然的方式，在改写文章时融入这些内容。我们主要寻找的是那些可以转化为几个额外段落的关键词。而“网站热度”看起来就是一个非常适合扩展的话题。其次，我们需要研究竞争对手页面能排名，但我们的页面却无法排名的关键词。为此，我将使用我们强大的内容缺口分析工具，这个工具可以在 Site Explorer 中找到。我们先从比我们排名高出两位的页面所覆盖、但我们的页面尚未涉及的关键词开始。这里有一些看起来很有潜力的搜索词：网站流量报告、追踪网站流量、主要网站流量。这些内容完全值得在我们的文章中用几个段落来展开，为读者带来实际价值。最后，第三步是研究与我们的主搜索词相关的其他热门搜索。为此，我们在“关键词 ADS”部分下准备了不少报告和关键词探索工具。此外，还可以使用 Google 关键词规划工具，它会建议许多值得考虑的有趣关键词。总而言之，我们的目标是研究人们围绕你的主要主题搜索的内容，并尝试基于这些知识扩展你的文章。实际上，这正是你最初就应该采用的文章撰写方式：研究人们的搜索内容，并根据这些搜索为你的文章创建大纲。这将有助于你未来的文章在这些搜索中获得排名，并从 Google 获取最大的搜索流量。这可能是我能给你的关于优化文章以适配 Google 的最佳建议。了解人们在特定主题周围的搜索内容，将帮助你为这些人创作一篇极具实用价值的文章。现在，你应该开始应用从本课中学到的一切了。

### 5.4 如何为你的博客内容审计

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1q34y1r7RA?p=9)　时长 2分57秒

**原文**

Part 4. How to perform an SEO audit of your published content. In the previous lessons I showed you how to find the amazing content opportunities with huge traffic and business potential. And now you also know how to squeeze the most traffic out of these opportunities. So you must be keen to pick a new topic and write an amazing new article for your blog, right? Well, let me stop you right there. If you're looking for a quick win, the best thing you can do is audit the content that you already have on your blog. And here's why. First of all, your published articles may already have some backlinks, which signals to Google that they are cool and deserve to rank. Well, the new article has to be promoted in order to get some websites to link to it. Secondly, your competitors most likely don't bother updating their content. So if the top ranking articles for your target keyword seem outdated, you can often outrank them by making your article up to date and republishing it as if it was brand new. Google will usually reward fresh content with high rankings. I see this happen quite often. And finally, your past articles might be simply targeting the wrong keyword, and therefore not bringing you any traffic. Figuring out the right search or intent and re-optimizing your article for the right keyword can do wonders to your search traffic. So here's how you audit your existing content. Go through every published article that you have on your blog and assign it to one of the three following groups. Group number one, update. These are the articles that already bring you some nice traffic from search. I'm pretty sure each and every one of them could be improved somehow, using the tactics that you've just learned. Even your best performing articles can be updated and improved. Take a moment to think about it. Most bloggers never bother improving their top-performing articles and that's a huge mistake, because the guys who rank below you are most likely working hard to train you. Group number two, merge. It doesn't make much sense to have multiple articles on the same topic. It's always better to have one great in-depth article other than 5 mediocre ones. Especially if those duplicate articles have some backlinks. You can use 301 redirects to point these backlinks to your main article on the topic and help you to rank better. Group number three, delete. This group is optional, but here it is Chef's blog we have deleted nearly half of our old articles. The primary reason for doing that was that these articles were massively outdated and didn't bring any search traffic at all. So deleting them didn't make any different tower traffic. However some people believe that getting rid of your underperforming content makes Google love your website more. I can't prove or disprove the statement, but if I were in charge of Google I definitely use something along these lines as a ranking signal. And that's pretty much it. I'm sure you have many great articles on your blog that could be further improved. Do it and you'll see a nice traffic boost in the next few weeks. And then you can go ahead and publish some new amazing articles using all the tactics and strategies that you've learned so far in this course. Speaking of writing amazing articles, that is exactly what we're going to cover in the very next lesson. Sounds good, then I'll see you there.

**译文**

第4部分：如何对你已发布的内容进行SEO审计。在之前的课程中，我向你展示了如何利用巨大的流量和商业潜力找到绝佳的内容机会。现在你也知道如何从这些机会中榨取最大流量了。所以你肯定迫不及待地想选择一个新主题，为你的博客撰写一篇精彩的新文章，对吧？嗯，让我先打断一下。如果你想要快速见效，最好的方法就是对你博客上已有的内容进行审计。原因如下：首先，你已发布的文章可能已经获得了一些反向链接，这向Google表明这些内容很受欢迎，值得获得排名。而新文章则需要通过推广才能吸引其他网站链接到它。其次，你的竞争对手很可能懒得更新他们的内容。因此，如果针对你目标关键词的排名靠前的文章看起来已经过时，你通常可以通过让你的文章保持最新，并像全新内容一样重新发布来超越它们。Google通常会用高排名来奖励新鲜内容。我经常看到这种情况发生。最后，你过往的文章可能只是针对了错误的关键词，因此无法为你带来任何流量。找出正确的搜索词或用户意图，并针对正确的关键词重新优化你的文章，可以为你的搜索流量带来显著的提升。以下是审计现有内容的方法：逐一查看你博客上已发布的每一篇文章，并将其归入以下三组之一。第一组：更新。这些是已经能为你带来不错搜索流量的文章。我敢肯定，每一篇文章都可以通过你刚刚学到的策略得到某种程度的改进。即便是你表现最好的文章，也可以进行更新和优化。花点时间想一想：大多数博主从不费心去改进他们表现最好的文章，这是一个巨大的错误，因为那些排名在你下方的人很可能正在努力追赶你。第二组：合并。针对同一主题撰写多篇文章并没有太大意义。与其有5篇平庸的文章，不如只有一篇出色的深度文章。尤其是当这些重复的文章还拥有一些反向链接时。您可以使用 301 重定向将这些反向链接指向您关于该主题的主要文章，从而帮助您获得更好的排名。第三组，删除。这一组是可选的，但在这里我们来看看 Chef 的博客——我们删除了近一半的旧文章。这样做的主要原因是这些文章已经严重过时，完全无法带来任何搜索流量。因此，删除它们并没有对流量产生任何影响。不过，有些人认为，清除表现不佳的内容会让 Google 更喜欢您的网站。我无法证实或证伪这一说法，但如果由我来掌管 Google，我肯定会考虑使用类似的方法作为排名信号。基本上就是这样。我相信您的博客上还有许多可以进一步优化提升的优质文章。动手去做吧，您将在未来几周内看到明显的流量增长。然后，您就可以运用在本课程中学到的所有策略和技巧，发布一些全新的精彩文章。说到撰写精彩文章，这正是我们将在下一节课中要讨论的内容。听起来不错，那我们下节课见。

### 6.1 为什么你的博客内容必须很棒

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1q34y1r7RA?p=10)　时长 4分51秒

**原文**

Welcome to the lesson number 6, how to create great content that promotes itself. Here's what we're about to cover. First of all, I will explain why your content has to be great in the first place. Then I'll give you some actionable advice on how to create the kind of content that people will care to read and share with others. And finally, we're going to talk about creating unique content that will help you to stand out. So let's go. Part 1, why your content has to be great. I already gave you a ton of great tips on keyword research and how to discover great topics to write about on your blog. And I'm about to teach you how to promote your content and how to build backlinks to your content so that it would rank high in Google and bring you passive search traffic every single month. But there's something in between, which is insanely important for your blog success. And that is, creating great content. I know I know you've probably heard this advice a thousand times and your first reaction would be, team, please, let's keep that and move on to the stuff that matters. But it feeds the stuff that matters. The quality of your content predetermines the very success of your blog. If your content is awful, all the promotion and link building tactics that I'm about to share with you won't work. As well as the other way around, if your content is stopped, you'll have to work a lot less promoting it. The word of mouth will do the job for you. So to recap, great content promotes itself, while awful content is awfully hard to promote. Let me give you a few good examples from HS blog. I'm sure you know Reddit. This is an online community where a lot of very smart people are hanging out, and they are very skeptical most of the time. So if there's something wrong with what you posted there, you can be sure that they will be very vocal about it. And here's the thread where someone has posted an article from HS blog. When I saw that it has 20 comments, my first thought was that Redditers were grilling us. But it turned out that they were actually super positive. And they didn't just say good things about that specific article that was posted, but about our blog content in general. See yourself. Can just be made that finds the blog post from HS really good reads. It shifts always seems to have some great posts out there. Yeah, I like their posts as well. I agree, I really like their stuff. So what did we do to get this raving reviews on Reddit? Nothing. We didn't submit our article to Reddit. One of our readers did that. We didn't ask our friends and co-workers to post positive comments. The Reddit community naturally enjoyed our content. As you can probably tell, we got quite a bit of traffic from this Reddit thread. And it all happened with zero intervention from our marketing teams. All we had to do is publish a great article on our blog, and the promotion on Reddit happened naturally. Here's another example. You may know the growth hackers community, where some people submit the content they enjoyed, and other check it out and the voted if they happened to like it. Then the most reported articles of the week are being sent via email to all members of the community, and there is hundreds of thousands of people. So here's one of those weekly newsletters where an article from HS blog scored at the number one position. This case is very similar to the one from Reddit in a sense that we didn't have to do anything to make it happen. It wasn't our employee who submitted this article to growth hackers, and all their votes were 100% natural. I didn't even know that our article was trending at growth hackers, until I received this email newsletter. Again, zero promotion was done to make it happen. All we did is publish the great article that our readers enjoyed and shared with others. But don't get me wrong. I'm not advocating against promoting your content on Reddit growth hackers or any similar communities that exist in your niche. It's called Publish and Pray Approach and it won't get you very far. I do recommend you to be active everywhere where your target audience is hanging out, and promote your content to these people whenever you get a chance. With these two examples, I was trying to illustrate how much easier it is to get traction at these communities when your content is objectively awesome. So instead of spending your time looking for secret marketing tactic that will help you to hijack these communities, you should instead put all this time into making your content as awesome as possible, because this is the single best way to conquer any audience and turn them into your friends. You go this to make your content so awesome that anyone reading it would want to share it with someone else. I love what Leo vidrich the co-founder of Buffer tweeted the other day. When writing a post, I get into a mindset to answer just this one question with a yes. Would anyone email this article to a friend? Way to often blogger's thing that their content flops because they don't promote it enough, while in reality their content flops because it's awful. In the words of Ryan Holiday, promotion is not how things are made great, only how they are heard about. So before you start promoting your new piece of content, please make sure that it deserves to be promoted in the first place.

**译文**

欢迎来到第 6 课：如何创作能够自我推广的优质内容。接下来我们将涵盖以下内容：首先，我会解释为什么你的内容首先必须是优质的。然后，我会给你一些切实可行的建议，教你如何创作出人们愿意阅读并分享给他人的内容。最后，我们将讨论如何创作能让你脱颖而出的独特内容。那么，我们开始吧。第 1 部分：为什么你的内容必须是优质的。我已经给了你大量关于关键词研究以及如何为你的博客发现优质写作主题的实用技巧。接下来，我还会教你如何推广内容、如何为你的内容建立反向链接，从而让你的内容在 Google 上获得高排名，并每月为你带来稳定的被动搜索流量。但在这两者之间，还有一个对你的博客成功至关重要的环节，那就是创作优质内容。我知道，我知道你可能已经听过这个建议上千次了，你的第一反应可能是：“拜托，别再讲这个了，让我们继续讲真正重要的内容吧。”但事实是，它恰恰是其他重要内容的基础。你的内容质量，从根本上决定了你博客能否成功。如果你的内容很糟糕，我接下来要分享的所有推广和链接建设策略都将无济于事。反过来也一样，如果你的内容足够出色，你就不需要花费太多精力去推广它——口碑自然会替你完成这项工作。总结一下：优质内容能够自我推广，而糟糕的内容则极难推广。让我从 HS 博客中举几个好例子。我相信你一定知道 Reddit。这是一个在线社区，聚集了很多非常聪明的人，而他们大多数时候都持怀疑态度。因此，如果你发布的内容有任何问题，可以肯定他们会毫不掩饰地表达出来。这里有一个帖子，有人分享了 HS 博客的一篇文章。当我看到它有 20 条评论时，我第一反应是 Reddit 用户正在严厉批评我们。但结果却发现他们的反馈非常积极。他们不仅对那篇被分享的文章给予了好评，还对我们博客的整体内容表示赞赏。你自己看看吧。有人表示他发现 HS 的博客文章读起来真的很不错。这个版块似乎总能发布一些精彩的文章。是的，我也很喜欢他们的文章。我同意，我真的很喜欢他们的作品。那么我们到底做了什么，才能在 Reddit 上收获如潮好评呢？什么都没做。我们没有把文章提交到 Reddit，而是由一位读者代劳的。我们也没有要求朋友或同事发布正面评论。Reddit 社区完全是自发地喜欢上了我们的内容。正如你可能已经注意到的，我们从这个 Reddit 帖子中获得了相当大的流量。而这一切都发生在我们的营销团队完全没有干预的情况下。我们唯一要做的就是发布一篇高质量的博客文章，Reddit 上的推广自然就发生了。这里还有另一个例子。你可能知道“增长黑客”（growth hackers）社区，在那里，人们会提交自己喜欢的内容，其他人则会查看并投票表达是否喜欢。每周被推荐最多的文章会通过电子邮件发送给所有社区成员，而这个社区拥有数十万用户。这就是其中一封每周简报，来自 HS 博客的一篇文章位列第一。这种情况与 Reddit 的例子非常相似——我们几乎无需做任何事情，它就自然发生了。这篇文章并非由我们的员工提交给 Growth Hackers 的，而且他们所有的投票都是 100% 自然产生的。在我收到这封电子邮件通讯之前，我甚至都不知道我们的文章正在 Growth Hackers 上走红。再次强调，我们完全没有进行任何推广来促成这一结果。我们所做的只是发布了一篇优秀的文章，而我们的读者喜欢并分享给了其他人。但别误会我的意思。我并不是反对在 Reddit 的 Growth Hackers 或你所在细分领域的其他类似社区上推广你的内容。这种方法被称为“发布并祈祷法”（Publish and Pray Approach），它并不能让你走得很远。我确实建议你在目标受众活跃的地方保持活跃，并在有机会时向他们推广你的内容。通过这两个例子，我想说明的是，当你的内容本身确实出色时，在这些社区中获得关注要容易得多。因此，与其花时间寻找能够帮助你“劫持”这些社区的秘密营销策略，不如将所有时间投入到让你的内容尽可能出色上，因为这是赢得任何受众并将其转化为你朋友的唯一最佳方式。你这样做是为了让你的内容变得如此出色，以至于任何读到它的人都会想要分享给别人。我很喜欢 Buffer 联合创始人 Leo Vidrich 前几天发的一条推文。在写文章时，我会进入一种只想用一个“是”来回答这个问题的状态：有人会把这个文章通过电子邮件发给朋友吗？博主们常常认为他们的内容反响不佳是因为推广得不够，而实际上，他们的内容反响不佳是因为内容本身很糟糕。用 Ryan Holiday 的话来说，推广并不能让事物变得优秀，它只能让人们知道它们的存在。所以，在你开始推广自己的新内容之前，请先确保它本身就值得被推广。

### 6.2 如何创建能被分享的高质量内容

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1q34y1r7RA?p=11)　时长 7分16秒

**原文**

For two, how to create content that people will care to read and share with others. Most resources that I've examined while preparing for this very course, will advise you to create great content and call it a day. I mean, what does it mean to create great content? And the content you create great already? And how do you know if it's not? Well, I don't consider myself to be a content marketing superstar, but in the last few years I managed to make a chef's blog one of the best ones in the SEO's pace, and I think along the way, I figured a few ingredients that will make your content great. Here they are, quality, uniqueness, and authority. Let me expand on each of them one by one, quality. In all honesty, there is no shortage of advice on how to improve the quality of your content. So I'll just do a brief recap of the things that I'm sure you're already well familiar with. Here they are. Number one, learn to write well, writing is a skill that almost anyone can learn and master. Think of playing a guitar. When you first try it, you surely can produce some sounds, but it can hardly be called music. But then you learn some chords, how to pick strings and so on, and after some time your friends are asking you to take the guitar and play them something. Same with writing, at first it will be very clumsy and tough to read, but after some learning and practice people will start enjoying your articles. Luckily there's no shortage of advice on the topic of how to improve your writing. Just start learning and practice as soon as possible. Now the next thing you do to ramp up the quality of your content is, improve how it looks. It is a well-studied fact that most people will skim through the article before reading it, which will help them to evaluate if it is worth reading at all. And if your article doesn't look good, people are unlikely to risk their time reading it. Here are the things that make your content visually appealing. The design of your blog, quality images, short paragraphs, subheadings, quotes, lists, and all sorts of formatting tricks. All these things make your article pleasing to a human eye and transmit a message to your blog visitors that the author of the article invested quite some time to ensure pleasant reading experience. But let's move on. The last but not the least, you need to craft an eye-cache headline. Headline is the only bit of your entire article that people will see on Twitter, Facebook, Google and on the homepage of your blog, which means you only have about a dozen words to persuade people that your article is worth their attention. Writing headlines that lure people into your article is both art and science. There are many ready-made headline templates that you can try using, as well as many psychological tricks that will educate you to create your own attention-grabbing headlines. I won't teach you any of that because I'm not a professional copyrighter, but there's one piece of advice that I can give you. Make sure to brainstorm at least five different headlines for every article you write. If you won't invest the time and effort into coming up with just five headline variations to choose from, your entire article is almost guaranteed to flop, no matter how good it is. In the words of Andrew Chen from Uber, titles are often written as a vague prethought, but in fact, it is the most important creative decision you'll make. And this wraps up my brief recap of ramping up the quality of your content. But in all honesty, all these things are just a proxy to the actual idea behind your article, which brings us to the second ingredient of great content. uniqueness. I see a rather upsetting trend in the blogging and content marketing space. When they go after some keyword, many people research everything that has already been published on the topic, and then try to squeeze it all into a single article of theirs. But this way you're only creating a clone of existing content and not adding any extra value. And if it's a clone, it doesn't deserve more attention than the original. So with this approach, you're immediately putting yourself one step behind your computation. If you want to stand out with your content, it has to be unique. So how to create unique content? Well, you still have to research everything that has already been published on the topic, but then you don't create a clone. You create something that would be different from what's already there. Ideally, you want to say something that hasn't been said before, which is rather hard to do as it requires you to be at the very forefront of your industry. These people are called thought leaders, and most likely you're not one of them just yet. So another option is to find a new angle, which would be somewhat different from the conventional opinion on the topic. Ideally, you'd want to turn 180 degrees and challenge the status quo. This often generates a lot of buzz, but only if you have good arguments to make your point. And finally, if the above is not an option, you might simply try to explain the topic better. You might not be the thought leader of your industry, but you might be a great writer, teacher, and storyteller. If you invest enough time into finding better arguments, better proof and better examples, you do have a chance to outperform the original piece on the subject. And now, here goes the last ingredient of great content. Authority. Like I just mentioned, in order to say something entirely new and unique, you have to be at the very forefront of your industry. You have to be an authority in your niche. Let's say I do a few weeks of research and write a cool article on the topic of colonization of Mars. Do you read my article on the topic, or would you prefer to learn about the colonization of Mars from someone with more authority in the subject? Like Elon Musk, for example. If you go to YouTube and search for his talks on the subject, you'll see that they get millions of views. That's because Elon Musk is probably the most qualified person in the world to talk about the colonization of Mars. So you know he can teach you a lot of things that you won't be able to find elsewhere. Hence, the crazy amount of views. And that is a nice illustration of the impact that authority and credibility have on the content that you create. But what if you're not the Elon Musk of your industry? Does that mean that you're doomed to oblivion? Not at all. There's a simple trick to hacking authority that writers have been using for ages. Take a look at this article at Wait but why? That explains the colonization of Mars start to finish. It is super long, so you'll need a rather compelling reason to spend your time on it, right? Well, how about that? This article was co-written by Elon Musk. I bet now you're thinking that every paragraph of this post is totally worth your time, right? That's how you hack authority. If you're not the best person to talk about a certain subject, find that best person and interview them. Obviously, it is almost impossible to get a hold of a person like Elon Musk and interview them for your article, but I'm sure that whatever industry you're in, there are many high-profile people who are easily accessible. Think about journalists. This is one of the core duties of their profession, to find credible sources and use them in the writing. If you want to create great content, you need to be a journalist. Here's an example of our own article at It Chef's blog, where we try to answer a question how to become a marketing manager. We could easily crank up an article on the topic in a few hours and call it a day. But instead, we took time to reach out to 10 high-profile marketers and use their advice to create a great resource on the topic. As you can see, we featured all 10 of them early in our article, so that to show our readers that there are quite a few very smart people behind what they're about to read.

**译文**

对于“如何创作出人们愿意阅读并分享给他人的内容”这个问题，我查阅了许多为这门课程准备资料时看到的相关资源，它们大多建议你创作优质内容，然后就此打住。但问题是，什么才算创作出优质内容？你创作的内容真的已经足够优质吗？你又该如何判断它是否优质呢？我当然不敢自诩为内容营销领域的超级巨星，但在过去几年里，我还是成功地将一个美食博客打造成了SEO领域最出色的博客之一。我认为在这个过程中，我总结出了几个让内容变得出色的关键要素。它们分别是：质量、独特性和权威性。接下来，我将逐一展开说明。首先是质量。说实话，关于如何提升内容质量的建议可谓汗牛充栋。因此，我只简要回顾一下那些想必大家早已耳熟能详的要点。具体如下：第一，学会写好文章。写作是一项几乎任何人都可以学会并掌握的技能。这就好比弹吉他一样。当你第一次尝试时，当然能发出一些声音，但这还远远称不上是音乐。但当你学会了一些和弦、如何拨弦等等之后，过不了多久，朋友们就会让你拿起吉他为他们弹奏一些曲子。写作也是如此，起初会非常笨拙，读起来也很吃力，但经过一番学习和练习后，人们就会开始喜欢你的文章。幸运的是，关于如何提升写作水平的建议并不缺乏。只要尽快开始学习和练习就好。现在，要提升内容质量的下一步，就是改善它的外观。一个经过充分研究的事实是，大多数人会在阅读全文前快速浏览一遍文章，这有助于他们判断文章是否值得一读。如果你的文章看起来不够好，人们就不太可能冒险花时间阅读它。以下是一些能让内容在视觉上更具吸引力的要素：你的博客设计、高质量的图片、简短的段落、小标题、引文、列表以及各种格式技巧。所有这些因素都能让你的文章看起来赏心悦目，并向博客访客传达这样一个信息：文章的作者投入了不少时间，以确保读者获得愉悦的阅读体验。不过，我们还是继续往下看吧。最后但同样重要的是，你需要构思一个引人注目的标题。标题是你整篇文章中唯一会在 Twitter、Facebook、Google 以及博客首页上展示的部分，这意味着你只有大约十几个词来说服读者你的文章值得他们关注。写出能吸引读者点击文章的标题既是一门艺术，也是一门科学。市面上有许多现成的标题模板可供尝试，也有许多心理学技巧可以帮助你创作出吸引眼球的标题。我不会教你这些，因为我并非专业的文案撰写者，但我可以给你一个建议：确保每写一篇文章都至少 brainstorm 出五个不同的标题。如果你不投入时间和精力构思出五个可供选择的标题变体，那么无论你的文章写得有多好，几乎都注定会失败。正如 Uber 的 Andrew Chen 所说，标题常常只是初步模糊的想法，但实际上，它将是你做出的最重要的创意决策。以上就是我关于提升内容质量的简要总结。但说实话，所有这些东西都只是你文章背后真正理念的一种代理，这也引出了优质内容的第二个要素：独特性。我在博客和内容营销领域看到了一个相当令人担忧的趋势。当人们针对某个关键词进行创作时，很多人会研究该主题下所有已发布的内容，然后试图把这些内容全部塞进自己的一篇文章里。但这样做，你只是在复制现有的内容，并没有增加任何额外的价值。而如果它只是一个复制品，那它就不应该比原文获得更多关注。因此，采用这种方法，你从一开始就比竞争对手慢了一步。如果你想让自己的内容脱颖而出，它就必须是独特的。那么，如何创作出独特的内容呢？你仍然需要研究该主题下所有已发布的内容，但之后你不能去复制它，而是要创作出与现有内容不同的东西。理想情况下，你希望说出一些前所未有、尚未被提及的观点，但这相当困难，因为这要求你站在行业的最前沿。这些人被称为思想领袖，而你现在很可能还不是其中之一。因此，另一个选择是寻找一个全新的角度，这个角度与关于该话题的传统观点会有所不同。理想情况下，你希望完全转变180度，挑战现状。这通常会引发大量关注，但前提是你必须有充分的论据来说明你的观点。最后，如果以上方法都不可行，你或许可以尝试更好地解释这个主题。你可能不是所在行业的思想领袖，但你或许是一位出色的作家、教师或故事讲述者。如果你投入足够多的时间去寻找更有力的论据、更可靠的证据以及更生动的例子，你就有机会超越关于该主题的最初文章。现在，我们来看看优质内容的最后一个要素：权威性。正如我刚才提到的，要想说出一些全新且独特的东西，你就必须处于行业的最前沿。你必须成为自己细分领域的权威。假设我用几周的时间进行研究，然后写了一篇关于火星殖民化的精彩文章。你读过我写的关于这个话题的文章吗？或者，你更愿意从该领域更具权威的人士那里了解火星殖民化？比如埃隆·马斯克。如果你去YouTube上搜索他关于这个话题的演讲，你会发现这些视频获得了数百万次观看。这是因为埃隆·马斯克或许是全世界最有资格谈论火星殖民化的人。因此你知道，他能教你许多在其他地方找不到的知识。这就是为什么观看量如此惊人的原因。这也很好地说明了权威性和可信度对你所创作内容的影响。但如果你不是自己行业中的“埃隆·马斯克”呢？这是否意味着你注定会被遗忘？完全不是。有一个简单的技巧可以“破解”权威性，作家们早已使用这个技巧很久了。来看看这篇来自“Wait but why?”的文章吧，它从头到尾详细解释了火星殖民化。这篇文章非常长，所以你需要一个相当充分的理由来投入时间阅读，对吧？那么，这个怎么样？这篇文章是由埃隆·马斯克合著的。我敢打赌，现在你会觉得这篇文章的每一段都绝对值得你花时间阅读，对吧？这就是你如何“破解”权威的方法。如果你不是某个特定话题的最佳人选，那就找到那个最佳人选并采访他们。显然，几乎不可能联系到像埃隆·马斯克（Elon Musk）这样的人物并采访他们来撰写文章，但我相信，无论你身处哪个行业，总有很多知名度高且容易接触到的专业人士。想想记者吧。这是他们职业的核心职责之一：寻找可靠的信源，并将其融入写作中。如果你想创作出优质的内容，就必须具备记者的思维。以下是我们 It Chef 博客上的一篇文章示例，我们试图回答“如何成为一名营销经理”这个问题。我们本可以在几小时内快速写出一篇文章草草了事。但相反，我们花时间联系了 10 位知名营销专家，并利用他们的建议，为这个话题创作了一篇极具价值的参考资料。如你所见，我们在文章开头就展示了全部 10 位专家，以此向读者表明，他们即将读到的内容背后，有众多非常聪明的人在支撑。

### 6.3 如何创建脱颖而出的独特内容

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1q34y1r7RA?p=12)　时长 3分46秒

**原文**

Part 3, how to create unique content that will stand out. As you can tell by now, I'm a big advocate of creating 100% unique content as opposed to rewriting what has already been published. If you will write about things that cannot be found elsewhere, your blog is almost guaranteed to be a success. But where do you find these unique things to blog about? I really like what drawing in hard, DC, O and founder of priceonomics said in his content marketing handbook. Authentic information that your company has access to is the currency of truly valuable content marketing. Sure, there may be more shareable content out there, cute animal pictures, snarky commentary celebrity gossip and delight, but that stuff won't get you customers. Information on the other hand will, it can be data that your company produces. Inside you have because of your industry experience, or stories about the people you have access to. Let's focus on the last sentence. You can craft unique content from the data that your company produces, insights you have because of your industry experience, and stories of the people you have access to. I do admit that it's easy for me to be an advocate of these things since they work at age traps. We have tons of data that we can study. We have tons of unique insights that we can share, and we have access to the best minds of the SEO industry. But what if you're a newbie blogger and you don't have these kinds of luxuries? Well, actually you do. Anyone can collect some unique data about their industry. Anyone can come up with unique insights related to their industry. Anyone can connect with industry experts and get a bit of help from them. I know this because not a long ago, I've been a newbie blogger myself. Take a look at this article that I wrote for my personal blog back then. I wanted to write a post about guest blogging, but there were a ton of articles on the topic already, published by well-known people with a ton of authority and the big audience. My only option to stand out among them was to do something entirely unique, which no one else did before me. Well, remember my advice earlier in this lesson, I decided to turn 180 degrees and challenge what all existing guides were saying about guest blogging. I wrote some guest articles before and they didn't see any of the outcomes, the told these guides were promising I would get. So I could easily write a cool article based on my own experience in guest blogging. But why would people listen to some random blogger with zero credibility, right? As we've just discussed, people want to learn from authorities. So I followed another advice from this lesson. I acted like a journalist and reached out to over 500 bloggers, big and small, and asked them to share some data with me on how their guest articles performed and if they were happy with their results. This whole venture resulted in one of my best articles to date. And even though it was published on my personal blog, which hardly has any traffic that article generated quite a buzz and got over 100 links from 60 different websites. And it generated a total of 10,000 visits to my blog and keeps generating a bit of passive traffic even years later. Would I be able to get this kind of result by writing an article with the same tips that can be found in other articles about guest blogging? Of course not, my article only performed so well because it was 100% unique. So I hope that my story will discourage you from creating new clones of existing content and encourage you to bring something entirely new to this world. Let's be honest, the vast majority of people are too lazy to do these kinds of things. But it's actually a good thing. The most steps you take towards making your content awesome and unique, the less computation you're going to encounter. And that wraps up the lesson on creating awesome content. And in the next one we're going to start talking about link building and the kind of content that attracts links sounds good, then I'll see you in the next lesson.

**译文**

第3部分：如何创作脱颖而出的独特内容。正如你现在所看到的，我强烈主张创作100%独特的内容，而不是改写已经发布过的内容。如果你撰写的是其他地方找不到的内容，你的博客几乎可以肯定能成功。但你要去哪里寻找这些适合写博客的独特内容呢？我非常喜欢DC、O以及Priceonomics创始人在其内容营销手册中所说的一段话：“贵公司独有的真实信息，才是真正有价值的内容营销的核心资产。”当然，网上可能还有更多易于分享的内容，比如萌宠图片、尖刻的评论、名人八卦等等，但那些东西无法为你带来客户。而信息则不同，它能带来客户——这些信息可以是你公司生成的数据，是你凭借行业经验所掌握的内部洞察，或是你所能接触到的人们的故事。让我们重点看看最后一句话：你可以从公司生成的数据、凭借行业经验获得的洞察，以及你所能接触到的人们的故事中，创作出独特的内容。我承认，对于我来说，成为这些方法的倡导者很容易，因为它们适用于“年龄陷阱”。我们拥有海量的数据可供研究，有大量的独特见解可以分享，还能接触到SEO行业最顶尖的人才。但如果你是一个刚入门的博主，没有这些优势，该怎么办呢？其实，你并不缺乏这些资源。任何人都可以收集一些关于自己行业的独特数据，任何人都能提出与自身行业相关的独特见解，任何人都可以联系行业专家并从中获得一些帮助。我之所以知道这一点，是因为不久前，我自己也曾是一名新手博主。来看看我当时为个人博客写的这篇文章吧。我想写一篇关于客座博客的文章，但当时已经有很多知名人士发表了大量相关文章，他们不仅权威性强，还拥有庞大的读者群。要想在众多文章中脱颖而出，我唯一的选择就是做一些完全独特、前所未有人做过的事情。还记得我在本课前面提到的建议吗？我决定彻底转变180度，挑战所有现有指南中关于客座博客的说法。我之前写过一些客座文章，但它们并没有带来任何预期的成果，那些指导手册曾承诺我能获得这些成果。因此，我本可以轻松地根据自己在客座博客方面的经验写出一篇精彩的文章。但问题是，为什么人们会去听一个毫无可信度的普通博主的意见呢？正如我们刚才讨论的，人们希望从权威人士那里学习。于是，我遵循了这堂课中的另一条建议：我像记者一样行动，联系了500多位大大小小的博主，请他们分享一些关于其客座文章表现的数据，以及他们对自己的成果是否满意。这次尝试最终促成了我迄今为止最出色的文章之一。尽管这篇文章发布在我的个人博客上——这个博客几乎没什么流量——但它却引起了不小的反响，从60个不同的网站获得了超过100个链接，并为我的博客带来了总计10,000次访问，甚至在多年后仍持续带来一些被动流量。如果我只是写一篇包含其他客座博客类文章中常见建议的文章，是否也能取得这样的成果呢？当然不可能，我的文章之所以表现如此出色，完全是因为它100%独一无二。我希望我的故事能打消你复制现有内容创建新克隆版本的念头，并鼓励你为这个世界带来真正全新的东西。说实话，绝大多数人其实懒得去做这类事情。但这反而是一件好事。你在让内容变得出色和独特方面迈出的每一步，都会让你遇到的计算工作量更少。以上就是关于如何创作出色内容的课程总结。在下一节课中，我们将开始讨论链接建设，以及什么样的内容能够吸引链接。听起来不错，那我们下节课再见。
